<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, call `load_workspace_dependencies` to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to Codex. Tool names use the term "thread" and Codex uses "task" in the UI. When providing user-facing responses, use "task".
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.

If explicit user input is required for another reason before progress can safely continue, do not use the `request_user_input` tool. Ask the user directly with one concise plain-text question instead. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.
</collaboration_mode>

<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.

Skip phases only when explicitly justified.\n\nWhen exploring the codebase, read `CONTEXT.md` (if it exists) to get a clear mental model of the relevant modules, and check ADRs in the area you\'re touching.\n\n## Redact\n\nThis skill has you show commands, outputs and captured artifacts. **Redact every secret first**: write `<REDACTED>` in its place. Captured artifacts carry auth headers: quote only the lines that carry the signal.\n\nIf the redacted output is not enough to diagnose the bug, say so and ask the user.\n\n## Phase 1: Build a feedback loop\n\n**This is the skill.** Everything else is mechanical. If you have a **tight** pass/fail signal for the bug (one that goes red on _this_ bug), you will find the cause; bisection, hypothesis-testing, and instrumentation all just consume it. If you don\'t have one, no amount of staring at code will save you.\n\nSpend disproportionate effort here. **Be aggressive. Be creative. Refuse to give up.**\n\n### Ways to construct one, in roughly this order\n\n1. **Failing test** at whatever seam reaches the bug: unit, integration, e2e.\n2. **Curl / HTTP script** against a running dev server.\n3. **CLI invocation** with a fixture input, diffing stdout against a known-good snapshot.\n4. **Headless browser script** (Playwright / Puppeteer) that drives the UI and asserts on DOM/console/network.\n5. **Replay a captured trace.** Save a real network request / payload / event log to disk; replay it through the code path in isolation.\n6. **Throwaway harness.** Spin up a minimal subset of the system (one service, mocked deps) that exercises the bug code path with a single function call.\n7. **Property / fuzz loop.** If the bug is "sometimes wrong output", run 1000 random inputs and look for the failure mode.\n8. **Bisection harness.** If the bug appeared between two known states (commit, dataset, version), automate "boot at state X, check, repeat" so you can `git bisect run` it.\n9. **Differential loop.** Run the same input through old-version vs new-version (or two configs) and diff outputs.\n10. **HITL bash script.** Last resort. If a human must click, drive _them_ with `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh` so the loop is still structured. Captured output feeds back to you.\n\nBuild the right feedback loop, and the bug is 90% fixed.\n\n### Tighten the loop\n\nTreat the loop as a product. Once you have _a_ loop, **tighten** it:\n\n- Can I make it faster? (Cache setup, skip unrelated init, narrow the test scope.)\n- Can I make the signal sharper? (Assert on the specific symptom, not "didn\'t crash".)\n- Can I make it more deterministic? (Pin time, seed RNG, isolate filesystem, freeze network.)\n\nA 30-second flaky loop is barely better than no loop; a 2-second deterministic one is tight, a debugging superpower.\n\n### Non-deterministic bugs\n\nThe goal is not a clean repro but a **higher reproduction rate**. Loop the trigger 100×, parallelise, add stress, narrow timing windows, inject sleeps. A 50%-flake bug is debuggable; 1% is not, so keep raising the rate until it\'s debuggable.\n\n### When you genuinely cannot build a loop\n\nStop and say so explicitly. List what you tried. Ask the user for: (a) access to whatever environment reproduces it, (b) a redacted captured artifact (HAR file, log dump, core dump, screen recording with timestamps), or (c) permission to add temporary production instrumentation. Do **not** proceed to hypothesise without a loop.\n\n### Completion criterion: a tight loop that goes red\n\nPhase 1 is done when the loop is **tight** and **red-capable**: you can name **one command** (a script path, a test invocation, a curl) that you have **already run at least once** (show the invocation and its output, redacted), and that is:\n\n- [ ] **Red-capable**: it drives the actual bug code path and asserts the **user\'s exact symptom**, so it can go red on this bug and green once fixed. Not "runs without erroring"; it must be able to _catch this specific bug_.\n- [ ] **Deterministic**: same verdict every run (flaky bugs: a pinned, high reproduction rate, per above).\n- [ ] **Fast**: seconds, not minutes.\n- [ ] **Agent-runnable**: you can run it unattended; a human in the loop only via `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh`.\n\nIf you catch yourself reading code to build a theory before this command exists, **stop: jumping straight to a hypothesis is the exact failure this skill prevents.** No red-capable command, no Phase 2.\n\n## Phase 2: Reproduce + minimise\n\nRun the loop. Watch it go red as the bug appears.\n\nConfirm:\n\n- [ ] The loop produces the failure mode the **user** described, not a different failure that happens to be nearby. Wrong bug = wrong fix.\n- [ ] The failure is reproducible across multiple runs (or, for non-deterministic bugs, reproducible at a high enough rate to debug against).\n- [ ] You have captured the exact symptom (error message, wrong output, slow timing) so later phases can verify the fix actually addresses it.\n\n### Minimise\n\nOnce it\'s red, shrink the repro to the **smallest scenario that still goes red**. Cut inputs, callers, config, data, and steps **one at a time**, re-running the loop after each cut, and keep only what\'s load-bearing for the failure.\n\nWhy bother: a minimal repro shrinks the hypothesis space in Phase 3 (fewer moving parts left to suspect) and becomes the clean regression test in Phase 5.\n\nDone when **every remaining element is load-bearing**: removing any one of them makes the loop go green.\n\nDo not proceed until you have reproduced **and** minimised.\n\n## Phase 3: Hypothesise\n\nGenerate **3–5 ranked hypotheses** before testing any of them. Single-hypothesis generation anchors on the first plausible idea.\n\nEach hypothesis must be **falsifiable**: state the prediction it makes.\n\n> Format: "If <X> is the cause, then <changing Y> will make the bug disappear / <changing Z> will make it worse."\n\nIf you cannot state the prediction, the hypothesis is a vibe: discard or sharpen it.\n\n**Show the ranked list to the user before testing.** They often have domain knowledge that re-ranks instantly ("we just deployed a change to #3"), or know hypotheses they\'ve already ruled out. Cheap checkpoint, big time saver. Don\'t block on it; proceed with your ranking if the user is AFK.\n\n## Phase 4: Instrument\n\nEach probe must map to a specific prediction from Phase 3. **Change one variable at a time.**\n\nTool preference:\n\n1. **Debugger / REPL inspection** if the env supports it. One breakpoint beats ten logs.\n2. **Targeted logs** at the boundaries that distinguish hypotheses.\n3. Never "log everything and grep".\n\n**Tag every debug log** with a unique prefix, e.g. `[DEBUG-a4f2]`. Cleanup at the end becomes a single grep. Untagged logs survive; tagged logs die.\n\n**Perf branch.** For performance regressions, logs are usually wrong. Instead: establish a baseline measurement (timing harness, `performance.now()`, profiler, query plan), then bisect. Measure first, fix second.\n\n## Phase 5: Fix + regression test\n\nWrite the regression test **before the fix**, but only if there is a **correct seam** for it.\n\nA correct seam is one where the test exercises the **real bug pattern** as it occurs at the call site. If the only available seam is too shallow (single-caller test when the bug needs multiple callers, unit test that can\'t replicate the chain that triggered the bug), a regression test there gives false confidence.\n\n**If no correct seam exists, that itself is the finding.** Note it. The codebase architecture is preventing the bug from being locked down. Flag this for the next phase.\n\nIf a correct seam exists:\n\n1. Turn the minimised repro into a failing test at that seam.\n2. Watch it fail.\n3. Apply the fix.\n4. Watch it pass.\n5. Re-run the Phase 1 feedback loop against the original (un-minimised) scenario.\n\n## Phase 6: Cleanup\n\nRequired before declaring done:\n\n- [ ] Original repro no longer reproduces (re-run the Phase 1 loop)\n- [ ] Regression test passes (or absence of seam is documented)\n- [ ] All `[DEBUG-...]` instrumentation removed (`grep` the prefix)\n- [ ] Throwaway prototypes deleted (or moved to a clearly-marked debug location)\n- [ ] The hypothesis that turned out correct is stated in the commit / PR message, so the next debugger learns\n\r\n'}]

Do not hand-edit generated types.\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\n\n## Stack And Commands\n\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\n- Build: `npm run build`\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\n- Test: `npm run test`\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\n\n## Working Rules\n\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\n\n## Product Constraints\n\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\n\n## Smart Drill Note\n\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\n\n## Verification (must not skip)\n\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\n\n## Workflow Expectations\n\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\n\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\n\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\n\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\n\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '# Guided Drills & Explanations\n\nThe practice domain: how LSAT U presents drills, the questions inside them, and the teaching that wraps each one. Covers the drill surface (`src/components/drill/`, `src/pages/Drill.tsx`), the question bank (`src/lib/questionLoader.ts`, `public/data/PT*-LR.json`), and the guided-drill catalog (`src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`, `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\n\n## Language\n\n**Question Explanation**:\nThe question-level teaching shown *after* a student answers one specific question. In the schema this is the `breakdown` (conclusion, conclusionSimple, evidence, justification, objection, prediction, crucialInsight) plus `answerChoiceExplanations` (per-choice verdict + whyCorrect/whyIncorrect) on an `LRQuestion`. One per question. Currently authored for **0 of 3,964** questions — this is the headline gap (Phase 2).\n_Avoid_: explanation (ambiguous), breakdown (that\'s only one half), answer key, rationale.\n\n**Drill Instruction Card**:\nThe drill-level teaching panel shown *before* a student starts a guided drill — teaches the pattern the drill targets ("these six questions all hide the conclusion in the first sentence..."). One per guided drill. Stored as `instruction_card: { left_md, right_md }` on a guided drill. The LSAT Lab equivalent is its "Info" card (captured verbatim in `[[LSAT-LAB-LR-INFO-CARDS]]` in the vault).\n_Avoid_: info card (that\'s LSAT Lab\'s name), intro, launch card, explanation.\n\n**Question Type**:\nThe LSAT task a question asks — *Main Conclusion, Flaw, Necessary Assumption, Strengthen, Paradox, Must Be True*, etc. Stored as `questionType` on a question (and `question_types[]` on a guided drill). The bank\'s raw values are messy (110 variants, casing dupes, 67 "Unknown") and need a canonical set (~25 types).\n_Avoid_: qtype in prose (fine in code), category (that\'s Patterns/Process), question kind.\n\n**Question Feature**:\nA finer-grained pattern tag layered on top of the type — *First Sentence Conclusion, Explain Curious Fact, Anti-Causal, Famous Flaws, Conditional Diagramming, Missing Link / Idea*. Stored as `questionFeatures[]`. 60 clean values; this vocabulary effectively defines the guided-drill taxonomy.\n_Avoid_: tag, pattern (overloaded), reasoning type (that\'s a separate field).\n\n**Reasoning Type**:\nThe underlying logical structure — *Causation, Conditional Logic, Comparisons*. Stored as `reasoningType[]`. Distinct from Question Feature.\n_Avoid_: using interchangeably with Question Feature.\n\n**Drill Signature** (proposed):\nThe accurate definition of what a guided drill drills = the set of Question Types + Question Features of the *actual questions in its pool* (its qids), NOT the types/features merely mentioned in its Drill Instruction Card text.\n_Avoid_: deriving a drill\'s targets from card prose alone (it over-captures).\n\n**Guided Drill**:\nA curated, fixed practice set of LSAT questions with a Drill Instruction Card and (target state) a deterministic ordered question list. 108 exist (`guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\n_Avoid_: drill (too generic — could mean Smart Drill), set, assignment.\n\n**Answer Navigator** (the "bubbles"):\nThe strip along the bottom of the official testing frame: one small empty **oval**\nper question in the queue, the question number printed *below* each oval, the\ncurrent question\'s number bold with a thin I-beam tick above it. Rendered by\n`OfficialAnswerNavigator`. Joshua calls the ovals "bubbles"; the code calls them\novals and the strip an answer navigator. Both names mean this one thing.\nOne oval stands for one **question**, never for an answer choice. This matters\nbecause "bubble" is overloaded: on paper, the bubble you fill in IS your answer\n(A-E). Here it is not. An oval is a place to go, so anything that turns it into a\nverdict on your answer is out of scope for it by definition.\n_Avoid_: pager (that is the RC per-question control that lives in the bar\'s\n`pager` slot), dots, progress bar (it is navigation, not a progress readout),\nand using "bubble" for an A-E answer choice.\n\n**Oval State**:\nWhat one oval in the Answer Navigator communicates. Today there are exactly three,\nand they are orthogonal: **current** (which question you are on), **answered** (a\nqid present in `attemptedQids`, meaning at least one recorded attempt), and\n**jumpable** (clicking moves you there, governed by `jumpEnabled`). Notably there\nis no correct/incorrect state: a section never reveals, so the strip has never had\nto say whether an answer was right.\n_Avoid_: "completed" or "done" (answered does not mean finished or correct),\n"selected" (that is an answer choice, not an oval).\n\n**Drill Launch**:\nEverything the runner needs to be told in order to start a drill: which kind of\ndrill, and the name of the particular run. It is carried in the URL and nowhere\nelse, so any drill can be refreshed, bookmarked, or reopened. A Drill Launch\nnames a run; it does not contain one. The questions, the answers already given\nand the position in the queue live in the Run Record.\n_Avoid_: route state, config (that word currently means two different things in\nthe code, a filter and a fixed question list), params, drill options.\n\n**Drill Run Spec**:\nA Drill Launch after its row has been read: the mode, the question list or the\nfilter that draws one, and the ids saying which stored row this run belongs to.\nBuilt by `drillRunSpecFromLaunch` and handed to the runner through context by\n`DrillLaunchGate`, which is the only way the runner learns what it is running.\nThe Drill Launch is what a link says; the Drill Run Spec is what the runner is\ntold. One is written and read from a URL, the other never touches one.\n_Avoid_: route state (the shape this replaced, and it read the URL a second\ntime), drill config (that word already means a filter or a fixed list), session.\n\n**Run Record**:\nThe durable row, per student per run, holding what a run actually consists of:\nthe ordered question list, every attempt, the position, and the clock already\nspent. Survives a closed tab, a sign-out, and a different device. It is the\nreason a Drill Launch can be short: the launch names the run and the Run Record\nsupplies its contents. A named generated run is keyed by that name, such as a\nBootcamp day and part or a Guided drill id, so a fresh draw cannot strand the\nrecord behind a different question list.\n_Avoid_: resume state (that is what it is for, not what it is), snapshot (that is\none write to it), session (overloaded: `DrillSession` is the in-memory object for\nthe current screen, and an auth session is a third thing).\n\n**Answer** (the pill):\nThe on/off control in the session strip that shows which choice is correct.\nOn: the correct choice\'s badge goes green, and if the student already submitted\na different choice, that one goes red. Off: both colours leave and the choices\nread as untouched. It says nothing about *why*; that is the Explanation pill.\nNeither direction locks anything and neither direction touches the logged\nattempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s Answer button ("Show correct answer" / hide).\n_Avoid_: reveal (the code\'s word; fine in code, but "reveal" sounds one-way and\nthis is a toggle), solution, show answer.\n\n**Explanation** (the pill):\nThe on/off control beside Answer that opens the whole Question Explanation:\nthe Argument Breakdown expander under the stimulus and a Choice Note expander\non every noted choice, all closed until the student opens them. Off folds all of it away and leaves the question exactly as it\nwas, including any Answer colouring already on. Never opens on its own: a wrong\nsubmit does not press it, only the student does. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s\nExplanation button ("Hide explanations").\n_Avoid_: breakdown (that is one half of what it opens), tutor, coach.\n\n**Argument Breakdown**:\nThe question-level half of a Question Explanation: the ordered labelled steps\n(Background, Evidence, Conclusion, Evaluate, Goal, and so on) plus the style\npills (Standard / Brief / Another Way) when the source published more than one\nstyle. Lives under the stimulus while Explanation is on, behind a chevron on a\nrule that the student opens, and is repeated in the drawer\'s Explanation tab.\n_Avoid_: explanation (the pill), breakdown steps, analysis.\n\n**Choice Note**:\nThe per-choice half of a Question Explanation: one choice\'s tag ("Out of\nScope", "Premise"), its "% of students", and its prose. Rendered as a dropdown\nunder that choice, closed by default while Explanation is on, behind a chevron\nbutton at the right edge of the choice card that the student opens and closes\nper choice. A choice with nothing published gets\nno dropdown at all, never an empty one.\n_Avoid_: inline explanation (the component\'s old name), answer explanation,\nrationale.\n\n**Reset Response**:\nThe control under the choices that clears the student\'s pick and nothing else.\nThe timer keeps running, the Answer and Explanation pills keep their state, and\nan attempt already logged stays logged. Greyed out until a choice is picked.\nA pick made after a Reset on an already-attempted question is practice, not a\nsecond attempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s Reset Response button.\n_Avoid_: undo, clear, retry (retry implies a new scored attempt).\n\n**AI Tutor**:\nThe generated coaching surfaces on the drill screen: the Coach chat that used to\nopen after a wrong answer, the "Ask Joshua about this question" hand-off on a\nquestion with no authored prose, and the "Explain your reasoning" voice chip.\nDistinct from the Question Explanation, which is transcribed LSAT Lab content\nand never generated. Off for every student as of 2026-09-12 behind one switch;\nthe code stays so it can be turned back on.\n_Avoid_: tutor mode (the code\'s flag name), Joshua (the chat\'s persona; a\nstudent reading "Joshua is having trouble" thinks the person is), coach (one of\nits tabs).\n\r\n'}]

The stimulus carries the extra weight of the serif\r\n  196:   // face; stem and choices stay on the sans stack at the same size.\r\n  197:   const baseSize = 19 * fontScale;\r\n  198:   const officialContentTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = {\r\n  199:     color: "#111827",\r\n  200:     fontFamily: \'"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif\',\r\n  201:     fontSize: `${baseSize}px`,\r\n  202:     fontWeight: 400,\r\n  203:     lineHeight: 1.6,\r\n  204:   };\r\n  205:   const stimStyle: React.CSSProperties = {\r\n  206:     ...officialContentTextStyle,\r\n  207:     fontFamily: "Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif",\r\n  208:     fontSize: `${19 * fontScale}px`,\r\n  209:     lineHeight: 1.7,\r\n  210:     // Fraunces is a variable font with an optical-size axis. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "import type { DrillLaunchBody } from '@/lib/drillLaunch';\nimport type { DrillRunSpec } from '@/lib/drillRunSpec';\n\n/**\n * How the official drill surface lets a student review a question.\n *\n *  - `fixed`: a fixed queue (guided drill, assignment, saved set, custom\n *    questions, practice set, bootcamp day, filter draw). Answer and\n *    Explanation are independent toggles, no Submit pill, nothing locks.\n *  - `adaptive`: Smart Drill. Same pills, but its own bar (Submit, Skip, the\n *    auto-reveal switch) stays.\n *  - `none`: a full section, a sealed run, or a practice-test leg. Today's\n *    coupled reveal, untouched.\n *\n * A pure function of the resolved spec, so an early render can never\n * misclassify a run the way the mutable `isPracticeSetMode` state could.\n */\nexport type ReviewPolicy = 'fixed' | 'adaptive' | 'none';\n\n/**\n * Which pick model a run uses. `list`: a pick is written straight into\n * `session.attempts` and the set is persisted at the end. `commit`: a pick\n * sits in `selectedAnswer` and is persisted per question on the way out.\n * Independent of the review policy, because a `questions` launch can arrive as\n * `natural-drill` and never sets a selected-qid list.\n */\nexport type PickModel = 'list' | 'commit';\n\nfunction policyForKind(kind: DrillLaunchBody['kind']): ReviewPolicy {\n  switch (kind) {\n    case 'guided-drill':\n    case 'assignment':\n    case 'saved-set':\n    case 'questions':\n    case 'practice-set':\n    case 'bootcamp-day':\n    case 'filter':\n      return 'fixed';\n    case 'smart-drill':\n      return 'adaptive';\n    case 'section':\n      return 'none';\n    default: {\n      const never: never = kind;\n      return never;\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nexport function reviewPolicyForRun(spec: DrillRunSpec): ReviewPolicy {\n  if (spec.sealed || spec.ptRun) return 'none';\n  if (!spec.launchKind) return 'none';\n  return policyForKind(spec.launchKind);\n}\n\n/** The exact condition the runner uses to enter the in-memory-attempt model. */\nexport function hasSelectedQidList(spec: DrillRunSpec): boolean {\n  if (spec.mode !== 'type-drill') return false;\n  const config = spec.config as { selectedQids?: string[] } | undefined;\n  return !!config?.selectedQids && config.selectedQids.length > 0;\n}\n\nexport function pickModelForRun(spec: DrillRunSpec): PickModel {\n  return hasSelectedQidList(spec) ? 'list' : 'commit';\n}\n\r\n"}]

[]), { answer, atMs }];\r\n 1419: \r\n 1420:         newAttempts.set(currentQuestion.qid, {\r\n 1421:           selectedAnswer: answer,\r\n 1422:           correct: answer === currentQuestion.correctAnswer,\r\n 1423:           timeMs: isPracticeSetMode\r\n 1424:             ? questionTimer.current.getTotalTime(currentQuestion.qid)\r\n 1425:             : Math.floor(performance.now() - questionStartTime),\r\n 1426:           timestamp: Date.now(),\r\n 1427:           confidence: existingAttempt?.confidence || null,\r\n 1428:           reviewDone: false,\r\n 1429:           // The record says whether the answer was on screen before this pick.\r\n 1430:           answerRevealed:\r\n 1431:             existingAttempt?.answerRevealed ||\r\n 1432:             answerSeenQidsRef.current.has(currentQuestion.qid) ||\r\n 1433:             false,\r\n 1434:           answerTrail,\r\n 1435:           // Every entry after the first is a change of mind. []);\r\n 1454:       if (current.has(key)) {\r\n 1455:         current.delete(key);\r\n 1456:       } else {\r\n 1457:         current.add(key);\r\n 1458:         if (selectedAnswer === key) {\r\n 1459:           setSelectedAnswer('');\r\n 1460:           if ((session?.mode === 'full-section' || isPracticeSetMode) && session) {\r\n 1461:             const newAttempts = new Map(session.attempts);\r\n 1462:             newAttempts.delete(qid);\r\n 1463:             setSession({ ...session, attempts: newAttempts });\r\n 1464:           }\r\n 1465:         }\r\n 1466:       }\r\n 1467:       next.set(qid, current);\r\n 1468:       return next;\r\n 1469:     });\r\n 1470:   };\r\n"}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 3.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\nname: code-review\ndescription: "Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes: Standards (does the code follow this repo\'s documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/spec asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \\"review since X\\"."\n---\n\nTwo-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:\n\n- **Standards**: does the code conform to this repo\'s documented coding standards?\n- **Spec**: does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / spec?\n\nBoth axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don\'t pollute each other\'s context, then this skill aggregates their findings.\n\nThe issue tracker should have been provided to you. If `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing, tell the user to run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills`.\n\n## Process\n\n### 1. Pin the fixed point\n\nWhatever the user said is the fixed point (a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc.). If they didn\'t specify one, ask for it.\n\nCapture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.\n\nBefore going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here, not inside two parallel sub-agents.\n\n### 2. Identify the spec source\n\nLook for the originating spec, in this order:\n\n1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.), fetched via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\n2. A path the user passed as an argument.\n3. A spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.\n4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn\'t one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".\n\n### 3. Identify the standards sources\n\nAnything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.\n\nOn top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below: a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:\n\n- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.\n- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation. Like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.\n\nEach smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:\n\n- **Mysterious Name**: a function, variable, or type whose name doesn\'t reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design\'s murky.\n- **Duplicated Code**: the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.\n- **Feature Envy**: a method that reaches into another object\'s data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.\n- **Data Clumps**: the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.\n- **Primitive Obsession**: a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.\n- **Repeated Switches**: the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.\n- **Shotgun Surgery**: one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.\n- **Divergent Change**: one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.\n- **Speculative Generality**: abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn\'t have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.\n- **Message Chains**: long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn\'t depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.\n- **Middle Man**: a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.\n- **Refused Bequest**: a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.\n\n### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel\n\n**Standards sub-agent prompt** should include:\n\n- The full diff command and commit list.\n- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full (the sub-agent has no other access to it).\n- The brief: "Report, per file/hunk where relevant, (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls: documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."\n\n**Spec sub-agent prompt** should include:\n\n- The diff command and commit list.\n- The path or fetched contents of the spec.\n- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn\'t asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."\n\nIf the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.\n\n### 5. Aggregate\n\nPresent the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings, because the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).\n\nEnd with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don\'t pick a single winner across axes: that\'s the reranking the separation exists to prevent.\n\n## Why two axes\n\nA change can pass one axis and fail the other:\n\n- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**\n- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project\'s conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**\n\nReporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.\n\r\n'}]
